#### Phase T2 — A new worktree, ready to work in

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| T2.1 | `[+]` popup: one field (the name, slugified to `[a-z0-9-]` and unique among the repository's worktrees), a base choice (current HEAD — the default — or the default branch), Enter creates. `WorktreeCreator` runs `git worktree add -b <slug> <folder>/<slug> <base>` through `GitLineHandler`, adds `/<folder>/` to `<commonDir>/info/exclude` once, records the base in `git config branch.<slug>.agenstormBase`, then prepares (T2.2–T2.5) and switches (T1.6), all under `withBackgroundProgress`. Git's stderr in a balloon on failure (branch exists, path taken). Slug tests | ✅ | **Minimal version 2026-09-30**, pulled forward at Roman's request: `+` opens **New Worktree**, a small dialog rather than a popup — `DialogWrapper` gives Enter, Escape and inline validation for free — with the name (`WorktreeNames.slug`: lower case, `[a-z0-9._-]`, dashes collapsed, ≤ 60, shown as the folder `.worktrees/<name>` and a branch of the same name; empty or taken names refused) and *Branch off* (current HEAD, default; the default branch when `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref origin/HEAD` names one). `WorktreeCreator` runs `git worktree add --no-track -b <slug> <main checkout>/.worktrees/<slug> <base>` through `GitLineHandler` — always under the *main* checkout, whichever worktree `+` was pressed in; `--no-track` so a branch off `origin/main` does not track it — then adds `/.worktrees/` to `<common dir>/info/exclude` once (`ExcludeFile`, pure) and records `branch.<slug>.agenstormBase`; `NewWorktreeFlow` does it under `withBackgroundProgress` in the app scope and hands the result to `WorktreeSwitcher`; git's stderr goes to a balloon. `GitBranchesCollection` carries an `@Internal` member, so an existing branch is left for `git worktree add` to refuse. The `ButtonsGroup.bind` DSL call is an inline function built for JVM 25 — each radio is bound on its own. Tests: `WorktreeNamesTest` (4). `check` green, 867. **Closed 2026-09-30** with T2.2–T2.5: `+` now seeds `.idea`, carries `.worktreeinclude` files, clones the heavy folders, switches, then runs the setup |
| T2.2 | `worktrees/carry/IncludeFileMatcher`, pure: `.worktreeinclude` in gitignore syntax; a file is copied iff it matches **and** git ignores it (the ignored list from `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory` on the main checkout, filtered by the matcher); with no `.worktreeinclude` the default is `.env*`. Tests on a fixture tree | ✅ | 2026-09-30. **Git does the matching** rather than a gitignore parser of our own: `CarryPlan` (pure) builds `git ls-files --others --ignored --directory -z --exclude-from=.worktreeinclude` (or `--exclude=.env*` when there is no such file) for the candidates and the same with `--exclude-standard` for what git ignores, and keeps a candidate that git lists as ignored itself or under an ignored folder — so a tracked `.env.example` or an untracked-but-not-ignored `notes.txt` never travels. The worktree folders, `.claude/worktrees`, `.idea` (T2.4) and `.git` are never carried. `CarryOver` runs it through `GitLineHandler(LS_FILES)` in the main checkout and clones each entry with `TreeCloner` (shared with T2.3: `/bin/cp -c -R` on macOS, `cp -R --reflink=auto` on Linux, a Java copy keeping symbolic links as links otherwise or when `cp` fails; never over something already there). `WorktreePreparer` calls it from the `+` flow between creating the worktree and switching to it, under its own background progress. With `--directory` git collapses a folder that holds nothing tracked into one entry, which is the same copy. Tests: `CarryPlanTest` (4), `CarryOverTest` (5, a real repository through `git` on the command line, so git's own matching is what is tested — including the cloner's folder, symbolic-link, no-overwrite and fallback cases). `check` green, 879 **Fixed after the T2 guardrail run (2026-09-30):** the default pattern is now `/.env*`, anchored — unanchored, git matched `.env.example` files inside `vendor/` (laravel/framework ships two), and carrying them left a `vendor/` stub that stopped T2.3's clone; the heavy folders now go first as well. `CarryOverTest` covers the nested `.env.example`. |
| T2.3 | `worktrees/carry/TreeCloner`: the heavy folders (setting `worktreesCloneFolders`, default `vendor, node_modules`) that exist in the main checkout and not in the worktree are cloned copy-on-write — `cp -c -R` on macOS (APFS clonefile), `cp -R --reflink=auto` on Linux, a plain recursive copy elsewhere or on failure — through `GeneralCommandLine` with a timeout, the fallback logged. #### Phase T3 — Status on the tabs

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| T3.1 | `worktrees/status/PorcelainStatusParser`, pure: `git status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z` → `WorktreeStatus(dirtyCount, ahead, behind, against)`; with no live upstream (none, or gone — git then omits `# branch.ab`), ahead/behind against `agenstormBase`, else the default branch, never the branch itself (`git rev-list --left-right --count <base>...HEAD`). Tests on captured outputs | ✅ | 2026-09-30. Six tests on output captured from git 2.55: every entry kind (`1`, `2` with its extra original-path field, `u`, `?`; `!` is not a change), detached, `(initial)`, a gone upstream, the base fallback, `rev-list` counts. The default-branch fallback is new: a worktree an agent made (`claude -w`) has no recorded base, and "its commits the default branch lacks" is what its tab should say. An untracked folder counts once (`-unormal`, git's default — `-uall` could walk thousands of files) |
| T3.2 | `WorktreeStatusService`: one status run per worktree in the background, at most one per worktree every 3 s, on `GIT_REPO_CHANGE`, on VFS changes under a worktree while the strip is showing, and on frame activation; busy from `ProjectBusyGuard` for worktrees loaded in the IDE, locked (plus the reason) from `GitWorkingTree.isLocked` for the rest — Claude Code locks a worktree while its agent runs | ✅ | 2026-09-30. `worktrees/status/`: `StatusTriggers` (pure: an event inside a worktree → the innermost one; under `<common>/worktrees/<id>/` → that worktree, by the admin id the snapshot now carries; anywhere else in the git dir — refs, `packed-refs`, the main index — → every tab), `StatusThrottle` (pure: a request inside the 3 s window waits for its end, never dropped), `StatusReader` (one `git status`, plus one `rev-list` without a live upstream; the recorded bases — one `git config --get-regexp` — and `origin/HEAD` read once until the next repository change), and the service: one worker, runs only while a strip panel is showing (a `HierarchyListener` reports it), the feature is on and there are two worktrees or more. `git status` is git4idea's `readOptional` command, so it runs with `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` and never writes an agent's index — no feedback loop through the admin-dir events. **What the VFS cannot see:** a worktree never opened in this IDE has no files in the VFS, so an agent's edits there raise no event until it touches git; for those, a tick every 5 s — only while the IDE is in front — asks for the *locked* ones not loaded here, each as often as `PollBackoff` allows (5 s while its status keeps changing, doubling to once a minute while it does not, so a lock left behind by hand or by a crashed agent costs one `git status` a minute), and re-reads the busy guards (EDT, non-modal, as the switcher does). The registry now loads each admin dir's files into the VFS (index, HEAD and `locked` changes raise events) and re-lists only on an admin dir coming or going or its `gitdir`, `locked` or `HEAD` changing, not on each index write. 16 tests (`StatusThrottleTest` 3, `StatusReaderTest` 5, `StatusTriggersTest` 3, `PollBackoffTest` 3, `WorktreeRegistryTest` 2); `check` green (911, then 920 with the backoff and T3.3). **Changed by the guardrail run (decision 86):** `claude -w` locks its worktree as `claude session <name> (pid N start <UTC date>)` and leaves the lock behind when the session ends, so `LockOwner` (pure, 2 tests) reads the pid and start from the reason and asks `ProcessHandle` whether that very process still runs — a lock whose process ended is never polled and `WorktreeStatuses.endedLocks` carries it to the tab; and a live lock is polled with the IDE in the background too (5 s, easing to 15 s while unchanged — the IDE often sits beside the agent's terminal), a lock naming no process only while the IDE is in front (5 s → 60 s). The busy guards and the ended-lock check run every tick, in the background as well: no git, in-process. `check` green (923) |
| T3.3 | Glyphs on the tab: `●` dirty (tooltip: *n* changed files), `↑n ↓m`, `⚙` busy or locked (tooltip: the guard's or the lock's reason); nothing when clean and even. Label tests | ✅ | 2026-09-30. `ui/TabBadge` (pure: parts and tooltip lines — "3 uncommitted changes", "2 commits ahead of main", "Busy: …", "Locked: …"; `NONE` when clean, even and idle; counts with nothing to count against are not shown) and `WorktreeStripPanel` painting them after the name — the dot in `FileStatus.MODIFIED`'s colour, the rest in the context-help grey — with the label's text staying the name, its preferred width growing by the badge, and the lines joining its tooltip (`HtmlBuilder`); `show()` keeps the labels when neither tabs nor badges changed, so a status tick does not reset a hover or a tooltip. `WorktreeStripService` combines the registry's and the status service's flows. README, CHANGELOG. 6 tests (`TabBadgeTest` 4, `WorktreeStripPanelTest` +2); `check` green (920). Then (decision 86): no `⚙` for a lock whose process has ended — the tooltip says "Locked: <reason>, a process that has ended" instead, even on an otherwise clean tab; `TabBadgeTest` 5 |

**Exit guardrails — Phase T3**

| Guardrail | Criteria (pass/fail) | Status | Actual outcome |
|-----------|----------------------|--------|----------------|
| Matches git | A file edited in a worktree by an external agent shows `●` within ~5 s; a commit shows `↑1`; `⚙` while `claude -w` runs in an external terminal, gone when it exits | ✅ | Claude, 2026-09-30, robot IDE on `demo-wt/app` (8 worktrees), at Roman's go-ahead; timings with the IDE **in the background**. At start every tab matched `git status`. A real `claude -w t3probe -p …` locked its worktree for the session as `claude session t3probe (pid 68643 start Wed Sep 30 15:48:15 2026)` **and left the lock when it ended** — which would have kept `⚙` for good; hence decision 86. With a process holding a lock in exactly that format (a `sleep` standing in for the session): lock → `⚙` 2.6 s, an edit in that worktree → `●` 2.6 s, a commit there → clean 6.3 s, the process ends → `⚙` gone 4.7 s; a commit in `try-x` (base `main`, not locked) → `↑1` in 5.2 s, as `git rev-list` says. Both ended locks (the probe, a fake `pid 4242`) show no `⚙` and say why in the tooltip. **Limit (§7):** an edit in a worktree that is neither locked nor ever opened in this IDE raises no VFS event (its files are not in the VFS), so its `●` waits for a git operation there or the IDE coming to the front (23 s in the run); for files the VFS has, the platform applies an outside change after 6–15 s |
| Quiet | Six worktrees, the strip visible, the IDE idle: no CPU churn, no `git` processes spawning in a loop | ✅ | Same run, eight worktrees (two with ended locks), strip showing, IDE idle in the background: **0 git runs in 90 s** (counted from the service's debug log, one line per run). A new lock is an event: two passes of 9–10 short runs 3 s apart (the snapshot, then git4idea's repository change), then one run of that worktree a minute later. The in-front idle case could not be driven — macOS keeps a background app from taking focus, so `focusProjectWindow` from the robot does not activate it; it differs only by the pid-less locks' backoff, which `PollBackoffTest` pins |
| Log | No plugin exceptions | ✅ | The clean session (18:32–18:39): no SEVERE and no warning from `com.pronskiy` while it ran; the SEVEREs at its end (`SlowOperations`, write-unsafe context) come from the robot's own scripted open, switch and close, blamed on the Robot server. `verifyPlugin` after T3.3: Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263, 1/9 deprecated and 75 experimental usages as before, zero internal. Earlier sessions of the run were muddied by the harness (a forced exit without closing projects; a second `runIdeForUiTests` that handed its path to the running instance and rewrote the plugin jar under it) — and in those, the strip switched worktrees on its own, app ↔ fix-login every ~1.5 s at 17:59 (§7); the clean session, with a caller trace on every switch, had none |

#### Phase T4 — Cleanup and merge back

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| T4.1 | *Remove*: refused when the worktree is dirty, has commits its base lacks, or is locked — the balloon says which and offers *Remove Anyway* after a confirmation naming what would be lost; the worktree's project is closed first (busy → refused with the guard's reason); `git worktree remove` (`--force` only after that confirmation); the branch is deleted only if merged (`git branch -d`); the main checkout cannot be removed. `RemovalPlan` pure, tests | ✅ | Never `--force` without the confirmation. 2026-09-30. `worktrees/cleanup/`: `RemovalPlan` (pure) — stopped outright for the main checkout, a loaded project a guard calls busy, a lock whose process still runs (decision 86: an agent at work), and a status git cannot read; otherwise `Ready` with the risks (changes → lost, `--force`; commits the base lacks → kept on the branch; on a detached HEAD, commits on no branch → lost; a lock naming no process → lifted); a lock whose process ended is lifted without asking. `WorktreeRemover` gathers the facts (the paths from `git status --porcelain=v2 -z`, new `PorcelainStatusParser.changedPaths`; commits against the recorded base, else the main checkout's branch, else `origin/HEAD`) and removes from the main checkout: unlock, `git worktree remove [--force]`, `git branch -d` — the lock put back if the removal fails. `RemoveWorktreeFlow` + *Remove Worktree…* on every tab but main's; the window showing the worktree switches to the main checkout first. **One change to the step as written:** instead of a refusal balloon whose *Remove Anyway* then opens a confirmation, the confirmation comes first and names what would be lost — the files (five, then "and N more"), the commits left on the branch, the lock — with *Remove Anyway* as its button; a clean worktree gets the same dialog with *Remove*, since deleting a folder should never be one misclick. Refusals stay balloons. 20 tests (`RemovalPlanTest` 5, `WorktreeRemoverTest` 5 on a real repository, `RemovalTextTest` 4, `PorcelainStatusParserTest` +1, `WorktreeStripActionsTest` +1 and updated); `check` green (939) |
| T4.2 | *Archive*: any WIP is committed to the worktree's branch (`git add -A`, `git commit --no-verify -m "wip: archive <slug>"`), then removed as in T4.1 without the unmerged check; the branch stays, and *New Worktree from Branch…* in `[+]`'s popup brings it back. Plan tests | ✅ | 2026-09-30. `RemovalPlan.archive` (stopped as removal is, plus `NoBranch` for a detached HEAD; commits the base lacks are no risk, a lock naming no process still is) → `Ready(commit, deleteBranch = false)`; `WorktreeRemover` commits first — `git add -A`, `git commit --no-verify -q -m "wip: archive <name>"`, a failed commit stopping everything before removal — then removes without `--force` and leaves the branch. *Archive Worktree…* sits beside *Remove Worktree…* and shares its flow and dialog ("… committed to the branch feat/tables as “wip: archive tables-q”, then its folder … is deleted; the branch stays"). **The way back:** there is no `[+]` popup to hang *New Worktree from Branch…* on — `+` opens the dialog directly — so the dialog gained a third choice, *Existing branch:* with the local branches no worktree has checked out, most recent first (`git for-each-ref --sort=-committerdate`); picking one fills in the name from its last segment until one is typed, and `WorktreeCreator.createFromBranch` runs `git worktree add <folder> <branch>`, leaving the recorded base as it was. Tests: `RemovalPlanTest` +1, `WorktreeRemoverTest` +2 (a failing pre-commit hook is skipped; a clean worktree is archived without a commit), `RemovalTextTest` +1, `WorktreeNamesTest` +1; `check` green (944) |
| T4.3 | *Merge Back*: `MergePlan` pure — squash (the default) or rebase; preconditions: the worktree clean (else offer to archive-commit first) and the base's worktree clean. Squash = `git merge --squash <slug>` in the base's worktree, the result left staged so the commit dialog — and Epic D's generate button — writes the message; rebase = `git rebase <base>` in the worktree, then `git merge --ff-only <slug>` in the base's. A conflict stops the flow, says so and switches to the worktree holding it. Success offers *Remove Worktree*. Runner over `GitLineHandler`; plan tests | ✅ | The base is `branch.<slug>.agenstormBase`, else the default branch. 2026-09-30. `worktrees/merge/`: `MergePlan` (pure) — stopped for the main checkout, a detached HEAD, no base, a busy project or a running agent's lock (a rebase rewrites the files under them), a base checked out nowhere or with changes (named), nothing to merge; else `Ready` and its `steps` (the WIP committed first as `wip: merge back <name>`, then squash or rebase + `--ff-only`). The recorded base is turned into its local branch when a remote-tracking name was recorded (`+` records `origin/main` for the default-branch choice), and the main checkout's branch is the last fallback. `MergeRunner` runs the steps; **no half-merge on the base, either way:** a squash that conflicts is undone there (`git reset --merge`) and the base merged into the worktree instead (`git merge --no-edit <base>`), so the conflict sits where the work is; a rebase conflict stays in progress in the worktree. `MergeBackDialog` (what goes where, the WIP note, squash or rebase) and `MergeBackFlow`: after a squash the window goes to the base's worktree with the Commit tool window open; after a conflict, to the worktree; both balloons after success offer *Remove Worktree*. *Merge Back…* heads the tab menu's worktree group. Two radios under *A click on a worktree tab* in the Worktrees group. `SwitchPolicyTest` +1, `WorktreesSettingsTest` +1. **Checked live** (robot IDE, 2026-09-30, at Roman's go-ahead): with the setting on, `app` → `fix-login` kept both open, the project tabs read `app, app:fix-login` and so did the window titles (T4.5 live too), and switching back brought `app` to the front with both still open. With it off, Roman's own clicks in the same session replaced the project each time — `t3probe` (its `.idea` seeded on first open), the main checkout, and a worktree `ewerw` made with `+` |
| T4.7 | The folders holding worktrees are left out of the Project pane, as `.idea` is; *Project Files* still lists them | ✅ | 2026-09-30, Roman: the main checkout "shows .worktrees folder. although it's excluded it is still part of project view. it should not be shown in project view, only in project files view. similar to .idea folder". With *Show Excluded Files* on (the default) `ProjectViewDirectoryHelper.shouldBeShown` lists everything in the project or excluded, and hides `.idea` there (`ProjectUtil.isProjectOrWorkspaceFile`) with no hook. `worktrees/ui/WorktreeFolderFilter`, a `TreeStructureProvider` (DumbAware, in `agenstorm-git.xml`), drops the `PsiDirectoryNode`s of `WorktreeExcludes.paths` — the same set T1.7 excludes; *Project Files* is a scope pane (`ScopeViewTreeModel`) whose nodes are not `PsiDirectoryNode`s, so it keeps them. `WorktreeFolderFilterTest` 2. **Checked live** (same run, *Show Excluded Files* on): the Project pane's `app` lists `.claude .cursor src vendor` and the files — no `.worktrees`, no `.idea`, and `.claude` opens empty; *Project Files* lists `.claude/worktrees`, `.idea` and `.worktrees`; no SEVERE with an Agenstorm frame |
| T4.8 | A worktree made elsewhere (a terminal, `claude -w`) is prepared like one "+" makes: on its first open (setting, on) and/or as soon as it appears (setting, off) | ✅ | 2026-09-30, Roman: "if I create a worktree from terminal or claude session?" — until then only its `.idea` was seeded, on first open — then "go with 1 and 2 both behind a settings one for each". `WorktreePreparer.prepareMadeElsewhere`: `.idea` only when it has none, `.worktreeinclude` files as `+` does, heavy folders only when absent altogether (`HeavyFolders.clone(onlyAbsent)` — an agent's own `vendor/` is never mixed with the main checkout's). **First open** (`worktreesPrepareOnOpen`, default on): the switcher's lazy `.idea` seeding became this, under a progress indicator, before the window moves; with the setting off it still seeds `.idea`. **On arrival** (`worktreesPrepareOnAppear`, default off — it can race a package install the agent starts in the same seconds): `WorktreeArrivals` watches the registry; `Arrivals` (pure) counts a linked worktree new since the last snapshot and made in the last 10 minutes, never the first snapshot or another repository's; it waits 2 s for `git worktree add` to finish its checkout. `Preparations.once(path)` makes `+`, the first open and every open project's watcher prepare a worktree once between them, the later ones waiting; removal forgets the path. Tests: `HeavyFoldersTest` +1, `PreparationsTest` 2, `ArrivalsTest` 2, `WorktreePreparerTest` 1, `WorktreesSettingsTest` +1. **Checked live** (robot IDE, 2026-09-30, at Roman's "yes, check both live"). The first try found a gap in T1.2: with the IDE in front and idle, a `git worktree add` from a shell never reached the VFS — `.git/worktrees/arr1` stayed unknown to it for over a minute, so the registry, and with it the arrival, never saw the worktree; `claude -w` in the IDE's own terminal is that very case. Fixed: the registry also reads `WorktreeAdminDirs.signature` (admin dir names, `locked`, `HEAD` times — one listing, no git) every 3 s while the feature is on and refreshes on a change (`WorktreeAdminDirsTest` +1). Then, IDE in front: `arr2` from the shell had `.env`, `vendor/` (same as main) and `.idea` 3.5 s later, git status clean, the window left on `app`; with arrival off, `open1` stayed bare for 10 s and was prepared on its first open (switch done in about 1 s); `own1`, given a `vendor/` of its own first, kept it untouched ("cloned nothing, carried .env"). No SEVERE with an Agenstorm frame |

#### Phase T5 — Borrowed from Air: bring the diff along, a limit, cleanup

Added 2026-10-01 at Roman's request after a look at the worktrees of 2026.3's bundled Air plugin (`com.intellij.air`, public alpha; its own UI texts and `AirWorktreeSettingsNew` in PS-263.5701.46). An Air session's *temporary worktree* copies the current uncommitted changes and untracked files into the new checkout, its settings carry a worktree limit per project (15, off by default; creation is blocked at the limit) and a cleanup after N days of inactivity (30, off by default — its confirmation warns that it can delete uncommitted changes). Three of those fit the strip; the cleanup here only ever takes a worktree that loses nothing.

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| T5.1 | **Bring my changes.** `[+]`'s dialog gets *Bring uncommitted changes (N files)*, shown only when the window's worktree has any and enabled only with *current HEAD* as the base (a diff against another base may not apply). The changes are **copied**, the source left as it was: `git stash create` in the source (a commit of staged and unstaged tracked changes, the working tree untouched) then `git stash apply --index <sha>` in the new worktree; untracked files not ignored (`git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z`) are copied, copy-on-write where `TreeCloner` can. Runs after `git worktree add` and before T2's preparation and the switch. An apply that fails leaves the new worktree as git made it and says so; nothing is retried on the source. `ChangeCarry`, pure over the git calls, tested against a real repository (staged, unstaged, a rename, a deletion, an untracked file, a binary file) | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Carry* row). Air's *Saving local changes… Applying local changes… Copying untracked files…*. `worktrees/carry/ChangeCarry`: `git stash create` in the window's checkout, `git stash apply --index` in the new worktree (unstaged if the index cannot be restored), then the untracked, not ignored files (`git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z`; a nested repository's folder is skipped); the source and its stash list are untouched. The count in the checkbox is `git status --untracked-files=all`, every untracked file on its own. `NewWorktreeDialog` shows *Bring uncommitted changes (N files)* under *Current HEAD* only when N > 0, enabled only with that base, commented "Copied: this worktree keeps them too"; `worktreesBringChanges` remembers the choice (decision 94: off at first). `NewWorktreeFlow` carries after `git worktree add` and before T2's preparation; a failed apply leaves the worktree as git made it, says so ("…made without your changes … They are still where you left them") and still switches. Plain copy rather than copy-on-write for the untracked files (usually few and small). 4 tests against a real repository (staged, unstaged, deletion, rename, binary, untracked, ignored; untracked only; a change that cannot apply; nested repository); verifier Compatible, zero internal; `check` green (1033) |
| T5.2 | **A limit.** Setting *Limit worktrees per repository to N* (`worktreesLimitEnabled` off, `worktreesLimit` 15 — Air's defaults). At the limit, `[+]` refuses before its dialog with a balloon naming the count and offering *Clean Up Worktrees…* (T5.3) and *Settings*. Counted: the repository's linked worktrees, whoever made them (the strip lists them all); the main checkout does not count. `WorktreeLimit`, pure | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Limit* row). Air counts its own worktrees per project; here an agent's `claude -w` worktree takes a slot like any other. `WorktreeLimit.reached` (pure); settings `worktreesLimitEnabled` (off) and `worktreesLimit` (15, 1–100) as one row of the Worktrees group — *Limit worktrees to [15] per repository*, the number enabled with the box; `NewWorktreeFlow` refuses before its dialog with "This repository has N worktrees, and the limit is L. Remove one to make another, or raise the limit." and *Change Limit* (the settings page); *Clean Up Worktrees…* joins the balloon with T5.3. 1 settings test (defaults, the rule, the field disabled while off); `check` green (1034) |
| T5.3 | **Clean up.** *Clean Up Worktrees…* (strip overflow menu and the T5.2 balloon) lists the worktrees that are safe to remove — a `RemovalPlan` with **no risk at all** (clean, nothing the base lacks, not locked by a live process, not busy, nothing running in its terminals — U4.1) — with their last activity (the newest of HEAD's reflog entry, the admin dir's index file and the last time the strip opened it), oldest first, all ticked; *Remove* takes the ticked ones through T4.1's removal (no `--force`, the merged branch deleted). The rest are listed below, greyed, with the risk that keeps each. **Automatic cleanup**, a setting (`worktreesAutoCleanupDays`, off, 30 — Air's default): once a day, the worktrees with no risk and no activity for that many days are removed, never one with changes or unmerged commits (unlike Air), and a balloon says which. `StaleWorktrees`, pure: facts + now → candidates and the reason each other one stays | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Cleanup* row). Air's *Automatically delete worktrees after N days of inactivity*, minus the risk it warns about. `StaleWorktrees` (pure): removable = a ready `RemovalPlan` with no risk and no window showing it; least recently used first; `stale` adds the age. Last activity, all in milliseconds: the worktree's creation, the mtime of its admin dir's `logs/HEAD` (every commit or checkout writes it; `git log -g --format=%ct` would have given the commit's time, and the T3 status polling touches the index, so neither serves) and the mtime of its `.idea/workspace.xml` (the IDE saves it while the project is open and on close). `CleanUpWorktreesFlow`: `gather` reads every linked worktree's `RemovalPlan` (busy guards of open windows, live locks, U4.1's terminals) in the background; `CleanUpWorktreesDialog` ticks the removable ones ("fix-login — last used Yesterday 13:05") and greys the rest with what keeps each ("removing it loses 2 uncommitted changes: …", "open in a window", a live lock); `remove` runs T4.1's removal without `--force`, so a worktree that changed in between is refused by git, not lost. Entry points: every worktree tab's menu (not only the overflow, as first written — it shows whether or not tabs overflow) and T5.2's balloon. `WorktreeAutoCleanup` (project service, started with the strip): with `worktreesAutoCleanup` on, an hourly check sweeps each repository once a day, whichever window gets there first, and a balloon names what went. Settings row *Remove worktrees unused for [30] days*. 4 tests (3 rule, 1 settings) + the menu tests; verifier Compatible, zero internal; `check` green (1038) |
| T5.4 | README + CHANGELOG | ✅ | 2026-10-01. README, Worktrees: "+" bringing the uncommitted changes, *Clean Up Worktrees* among the tab menu's endings, and the limit and the automatic cleanup in the options paragraph. CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` *Added*: one entry for the three |

**Later, deliberately not specified in detail:** ports per worktree (a block exported to terminals and run configurations, Conductor's `CONDUCTOR_PORT` and worktrunk's `hash_port`); PR and CI badges on the tabs, and a worktree from a PR (263 has `Github.PullRequest.OpenInWorktree`); a teardown script; living alongside Air once it leaves alpha — its worktrees already show in the strip; what could collide is the strip swapping the window into an Air worktree Air expects in a window of its own, and Air's cleanup deleting one the strip shows.

### Epic U — Terminals that outlive the project  ·  after Epic T

**Goal:** a terminal that is running something — an agent, `npm run dev`, a long test run — survives the worktree switch and shows up in the new window's Terminal tool window, attached to the same process, instead of either dying with the old project or keeping that project open behind the new window. With the setting on and tmux installed, each IDE terminal tab of a Git project runs its shell inside a tmux session on Agenstorm's own socket; the switch hands the sessions that are running something to the new project and lets the idle ones go; a project opened after an IDE restart picks its repository's running sessions back up.

**Success metrics:** after `app` → `fix-login`, every tab of `app` that was running something is a tab of `fix-login`'s window within 2 s of the switch, its process the same (same pid, output continuing), and `app` closes with no dialog; no idle tmux session is left behind; with the setting off, or without tmux, nothing changes from today; zero internal API, `verifyPlugin` Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263.

Roman, 2026-10-01: "one major thing still not working: terminal sessions need to be terminated when opening a worktree. But I actually want terminal sessions be untied from project so that when I open a worktree, the terminal sessions are just transferred to new project." Asked in the brainstorm the same day he chose: only the terminals running something must move — an idle shell may simply open fresh in the new worktree (decision 88) — and the tmux-backed approach over the platform's own transferable sessions, IDE-held sessions or no transfer at all (decision 87).

**Why tmux.** Every terminal engine ties its process to its project: the reworked terminal's sessions live in the project's `TerminalSessionsManager`, which closes them with the project, and a classic tab disposes its `TtyConnector`. 2026.3 has the real thing — `TransferableTerminalSession` (`intellij.terminal.frontend`, `bind`/`prepareBind(project)`, a runtime scope of its own, prepare/commit/rollback bindings) — but every piece is `@ApiStatus.Internal`, nothing public uses it yet, and 2026.2 lacks it; the zero-internal rule (§2) rules it out. An app-level holder of our own (pty4j + classic JediTerm tabs fed through `TerminalWidget.connectToTty`) is public API, but only for tabs Agenstorm opens itself, on the classic engine, and gone with the IDE. tmux holds the processes outside the IDE altogether: closing a tab kills only the tmux client, the session stays, and any tab — in any window, after a restart, or in an external terminal — can attach to it. Claude Code already runs worktree sessions in tmux (`claude -w --tmux`).

Platform facts (checked 2026-10-01 in PS-262.10968.76 and PS-263.5701.46):

- **The command can be replaced — but not where first thought** (corrected by U0, 2026-10-01): `MutableShellExecOptions.setExecCommand` is itself `@ApiStatus.Internal` in 262 and 263, and so is `ShellExecCommandImpl`, the only way to make a `ShellExecCommand`. The public way is the older `localTerminalCustomizer` EP: `LocalTerminalCustomizer.customizeCommandAndEnvironment(project, workingDirectory, command, envs, eelDescriptor)` returns the command to run. The class is plain `@Deprecated` (no `forRemoval`, not internal), JetBrains' own split-mode customizers still extend it, and both builds' backend (`TerminalExecOptionsCustomizationRemoteApiImplKt.customizeShellExecOptions`) apply its result. The order in `LocalTerminalDirectRunner.configureStartupOptions` is: shell integration injected into the command first (`bash --rcfile …/bash-integration.bash -i`, zsh's `ZDOTDIR`), then every `LocalTerminalCustomizer`, then every `ShellExecOptionsCustomizer` (Epic G's shims) on the same environment map. So a wrapped command keeps the integrated shell inside tmux, and Epic G's variables reach the tmux *client*, not the session.
- **Tabs can be opened with a command:** `TerminalToolWindowTabsManager.createTabBuilder().shellCommand(List<String>)` is public (`@Experimental`, 262 and 263; only `emulatorType`, `shouldAddToToolWindow`, `sourceNavigationProject…` and `startupFusInfo` are internal), so a hand-off tab simply runs `tmux attach-session -t <name>` — no environment flag for the customizer to read. Such a tab still passes through the customizers (ours must let a `tmux` command through), and the platform restores it on reopen with the same command unless `restoreOnProjectReopen(false)`.
- **Not for us:** `TransferableTerminalSession`, `TransferableTerminalSessionFactory`, `TerminalSessionsManager` (all `@Internal`, the first two 263-only), `MutableShellExecOptions.setExecCommand`, `ShellExecCommandImpl`.
- **tmux is not everywhere:** macOS ships none (only GNU `screen` 4.0 in `/usr/bin`); Homebrew puts it in `/opt/homebrew/bin` or `/usr/local/bin`; there is none on Windows.
- **The close prompt** (checked 2026-10-01 after Roman's "can U1.6 reuse the current mechanism?"): a terminal tab's *terminate?* on ✕ and on project close comes from `TerminalTabCloseListener` (`@Internal`), which asks the tab's process for child processes — a tmux client has none, so it never asks. It is built on the public `BaseContentCloseListener` (`closeQuery(content, projectClosing)`, both ✕ and project close, as a `ContentManagerListener` and `VetoableProjectManagerListener`) and shows the public `TerminateRemoteProcessDialog.show(project, title, processHandler)`, whose answers are *Terminate*, *Disconnect* (leave it running) and *Cancel*. A listener of our own on the same base shows the same dialog.
- **Tab titles:** a tab shows the program's own title (OSC 0/2 — Claude Code animates `✳ Claude Code` there; 2026.3's `intellij.terminal.agentsProvider` only launches agents) only while the IDE thinks a command runs: `TerminalTitleUtils.shouldShowAppTitle` under the default *when command is running* asks shell integration, silenced inside tmux, or a `NON_SHELL` process type. tmux forwards a pane's title live with `set-titles on` + `set-titles-string "#{pane_title}"` (a direct pty capture got all 10 changes at 0.2 s intervals), defaulting to the hostname until a program sets one. Public levers: `createTabBuilder().processType(…)`, and `TerminalView.getTitle()` → `TerminalTitle` (`userDefinedTitle` — a rename — `defaultTitle`, `change {}`, `addTitleListener`), all in 262 and 263.

Package `terminal/tmux/`, registered in `agenstorm-terminal.xml`; the hand-off message and the repository lookup live in `core/` so `worktrees/` and `terminal/` keep importing only `core/`.

#### Phase U0 — Spike: does the reworked terminal carry a tmux session?

Throwaway code in the robot IDE; each answer recorded here, and a no on 1 or 2 stops the epic for a rethink.

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| U0.1 | Wrapped shell: a customizer replacing the exec command with `tmux -L agenstorm -f <conf> new-session …`; colours, mouse, resize (`window-size latest`), clickable `path:line` links, and whether the terminal's shell-integration environment (and its escape codes) is harmless inside tmux | ✅ | 2026-10-01, robot IDE (PS-263.5701.46, tmux 3.7c), through a `LocalTerminalCustomizer` — `setExecCommand` turned out `@Internal` (platform facts above). Tabs from the tab builder and from the *New Tab* action both came up as `tmux -u -L agenstorm -f <conf> new-session -s <name> -c <dir> -- <integrated shell>`; the client reports `xterm-256color` with RGB, hyperlinks, clipboard and focus. 16/256/truecolour, bold and underline render as in a plain tab. `src/Login.php:5` is a file link and an OSC 8 link passes through (the IDE's hyperlink highlighters, same as the plain tab). Resizing the tool window resizes the pane (108×14 → 108×25). The integration inside tmux is harmless — nothing visible — but inert: tmux drops its OSC events, so the tab's shell integration never initialises (no command tracking, no completion popup, the IDE's idea of the cwd stays the start directory). tmux's `set-titles` does reach the tab's title model (`application=bash`) |
| U0.2 | Close: a project whose tab runs `tmux` (the client) closes with no "terminate?" dialog, and the session survives detached; if not, detaching the client first (`tmux detach-client`) is the way | ✅ | Decisive — passes. Control first: `sleep 600` in a *plain* tab → `closeAndDispose` shows "Process 'app' Is Running — terminate?". Then with `sleep 600` only inside the tmux tab: no dialog, `closeAndDispose` returned true, both sessions detached with `sleep` still running under the same pid (81142) — the client has no child processes, which is what the platform's close check looks at. No detach step needed |
| U0.3 | Attach: a tab from `createTabBuilder().envVariables(AGENSTORM_TMUX_ATTACH=…)` reaches the customizer with that variable in `getEnvs()`, so it can become `attach -t` | ✅ | Decisive for U2 — passes, more simply than planned: `createTabBuilder().shellCommand([tmux, -L, agenstorm, attach-session, -t, u0-1])` in the `fix-login` worktree's window showed `app`'s session as it was — same screen, `sleep 600` still running, same pid, pane resized to the new tab; the customizer let the `tmux` command through. No environment flag needed. Closing and reopening `fix-login` restored that tab with its `attach` command and re-attached by itself; the wrapped *New Tab* tabs came back as fresh shells, leaving their old sessions detached (U2/U3 must reckon with both) |
| U0.4 | Environment: Epic G's `open`/`$EDITOR` shims and PATH reach the shell inside the session (`new-session -e`, tmux 3.2+, or `update-environment`) | ✅ | Half. The shims' paths reach every session (`open` and `agenstorm-edit` first on PATH, `$VISUAL`/`$EDITOR` set) — they are IDE-wide. The endpoint does not: `AGENSTORM_OPEN_PORT`/`_TOKEN` are per project, and a session takes its environment from the tmux server, which froze the environment of whichever client started it — every session carried `app`'s port 60178, dead once `app` closed, while a plain tab of `fix-login` had its own 60502. `-e` from the customizer cannot fix it: Epic G's customizer runs *after* ours. And a moved session would carry the old project's port anyway. So the shims must find the endpoint through a stable file, not the environment — U3.2 moves forward into U2 (as U2.4) |
| U0.5 | Scrollback: tmux's alternate screen hides history from the IDE; try `terminal-overrides …smcup@:rmcup@`, else settle on tmux's own (mouse wheel into copy mode) | ✅ | tmux's own. A tmux tab lives in the alternate screen (after `seq 1 300`: 13 lines against the plain tab's 309). `smcup@:rmcup@` keeps it on the normal screen but loses lines — 104 of 300 reached the IDE's buffer, 7 gaps where tmux redrew instead of scrolling — so it is out. The mouse wheel reaches tmux (`mouse on`) and scrolls its history in copy mode (20/299). Copying: the reworked terminal ignores OSC 52 (plain tabs too), so tmux's `set-clipboard` cannot reach the clipboard; `copy-command pbcopy` does (a copy-mode line landed in `pbpaste`). Whether a mouse drag in a tmux tab selects (tmux) and whether Shift+drag gives the IDE's own selection could not be driven — synthetic drags reached neither — so U1's guardrail checks it by hand |

| Guardrail | Criteria (pass/fail) | Status | Actual outcome |
|-----------|----------------------|--------|----------------|
| Go / no-go | U0.1–U0.3 pass, or a workaround is found for each; the verdict and the settled tmux config recorded here and as a decision | ✅ | **Go** (2026-10-01), decision 90 (confirmed by Roman the same day). U0.1–U0.3 pass on public API once the hook is `LocalTerminalCustomizer` + `shellCommand`; U0.4 needs the stable endpoint file (now U2.4); U0.5 settles on tmux's history. Settled config, started with `tmux -u -L agenstorm -f <conf>`: `status off`, `mouse on`, `history-limit 50000`, `window-size latest`, `aggressive-resize on`, `escape-time 0`, `focus-events on`, `set-titles on` + `set-titles-string '#{pane_current_command}'`, `copy-command pbcopy` (macOS), `default-terminal tmux-256color`, `terminal-features ',xterm-256color:RGB:hyperlinks:clipboard'`; no `smcup@` override. The robot log held no Agenstorm error (only the robot's own write-unsafe `openOrImport`). Throwaway code deleted, nothing committed but this record. Also seen: `TerminalToolWindowManager.getTerminalWidgets()` returned no reworked tab at all — neither the tab builder's nor the robot-invoked *New Tab*'s — so `TerminalCommandGuard` saw nothing, even with `sleep` in a plain tab (§7). **Demo for Roman** (same day, throwaway again, a project-open stand-in for U2's hand-off): Claude Code moved `app` ↔ `fix-login` five times with idle tabs ending; mouse selection works; three findings — the moved session's width short until a manual resize (→ U2.2), the stand-in missing `+` (→ U2's *Moves* covers `+`), and sessions outliving every tab (→ decision 91, Phase U3) |

Tests on captured output | ✅ | 2026-10-01. One `list-panes -a -F` call, fields split by the ASCII unit separator: per session its name, clients, creation time and `@agenstorm_repo`/`_project`/`_background`, per pane its pid, foreground command, path and title — with the host name alongside, so tmux's default title is not taken for a program's. `running` = a non-shell in the foreground (login shells' `-zsh` count as shells); `label` = the running program's title, else its command (Claude reports `2.1.286` as its command and `✳ Claude Code` as its title). 5 tests on output captured from tmux 3.7c; `check` green (984) |

The `-e`s carry this tab's environment — zsh's `ZDOTDIR` included — since a session otherwise gets the server's, frozen by the first tab (U0.4); Epic G's endpoint still comes later (U2.4). Names: the first free number, remembered within the run so two tabs at once do not collide. 4 tests, one with a real attached client through pty4j: the session tagged, its environment set, gone once the client is. `verifyPlugin`: Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263, **zero internal**; deprecated 4 on 262 / 12 on 263 (+3, `LocalTerminalCustomizer`, as decision 90 expected), experimental 79. `check` green (988) |

That dialog is the platform's rebuilt from public parts, since `TerminateRemoteProcessDialog.show` returns different types in 262 and 263: `ExecutionBundle`'s texts, *Terminate* / *Cancel*, and `GeneralSettings.processCloseConfirmation` as its *Don't ask again*. Only tmux tabs get the listener, and it opts out of project close — the base class would otherwise close a plain tab's process before the platform's own project check could ask. 6 tests (session names from commands, the dialog's remembered choice and buttons); `check` green (992) |

A rename (`userDefinedTitle`) wins throughout. 2 tests; `check` green (994) |

#### Phase U2 — The hand-off on a worktree switch

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| U2.1 | `core/ProjectHandOff`, an application topic `handOff(from, to)`; the worktree switcher fires it after the target opened and before it closes the current project (not when the current stays open) | ✅ | 2026-10-01. `core/ProjectHandOff`, first a topic, the same day an extension point (`com.pronskiy.agenstorm.projectHandOff`) with a `suspend fun handOff(from, to)` the switch awaits — a topic cannot be waited for, and U2.2 must finish before the close. `SwitchRunner` calls `Env.handOff(current, target)` after opening and presenting the target, right before closing the current worktree — and only when it closes it (not when it is busy and left behind, nor with *keep the current one open*). 1 new test, 3 updated (the call order); `check` green (995) |
| U2.2 | `TerminalHandOff`: for each session of `from` running something, a reworked tab in `to` from `createTabBuilder().shellCommand(tmux attach-session -t <name>)`, opened as `processType(NON_SHELL)` so the IDE shows the program's forwarded title (Claude's spinner included), keeping the old tab's rename (`userDefinedTitle`) and otherwise named from the pane title, else the command (Claude reports its version, `2.1.286`, as its command); attached **before** `from` closes so the session never drops to zero clients; `from`'s idle sessions then end by themselves (`destroy-unattached`, decision 91). The tab starts its client only once shown, at the size it is shown at; the window then follows the tab (`window-size latest`) | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Moves*). `TerminalHandOff` (registered on `projectHandOff`): the closing project's tmux tabs (their started commands, `TmuxShellCommand.sessionOf`), through `TmuxHandOffPlan` — running sessions only, each once, of the same repository — get `destroy-unattached off` and `@agenstorm_project` = the new project before the close, then a tab each in the new window: `attach-session -t =<name>` (exact, so `app-1` never matches `app-10`), a shell tab (first `NON_SHELL`; the guardrail run moved it back), started when shown (the size fix), the rename kept, else the old tab's own name (`TmuxTitleMirror.ORIGINAL_NAME`, not a spinner frame). A watcher turns `destroy-unattached` back on once the new client is there and unsets the window's `window-size` (2026-10-08 fix: the `resize-window -A` it used to run sets `window-size manual`, freezing the window at the tab's size of that moment, so a tab that grew — maximized, a taller tool window — showed tmux's dots below or beside it; `TmuxHandOffTmuxTest.aSettledWindowKeepsFollowingItsTabsSize`); a tab closed unstarted, or a window closed first, leaves a background terminal. 262's tab builder has no `restoreOnProjectReopen`, so it is not called: the platform restores the attach tab on reopen in both builds — harmless now that session names carry a random suffix (a stale tab finds no session and closes), and the way back for a kept one (U3.1). 5 tests; verifier Compatible, zero internal (experimental 143); `check` green (999) |
| U2.3 | `TerminalCommandGuard` no longer counts a tmux-backed tab as busy — its process survives the close — so the switch closes `from` instead of leaving it behind | ✅ | 2026-10-01. The blind spot was wider than tmux: `TerminalToolWindowManager.getTerminalWidgets()` lists only the tabs made through the old manager, so `TerminalCommandGuard` never saw a reworked tab at all — a plain one running `sleep` counted as idle (U0). It now also asks `TerminalToolWindowTabsManager`: a tab is busy while shell integration reports `ExecutingCommand` or while a non-shell program runs in it — never when its process is a tmux client. No tabs while the project has no Terminal tool window (the manager throws then). This also changes Epic P's offloading and Epic T's switch for plain reworked tabs: a command running there now keeps the project, as intended. 3 tests; verifier Compatible, zero internal (experimental 164); `check` green (1002) |
| U2.4 | The `open`/`$EDITOR` shims find the IDE's endpoint (port, token) through a stable file the IDE keeps current, the environment only as a fallback, so a session made in or moved from another project — or one that outlived a restart — still reaches the IDE | ✅ | 2026-10-01. `TerminalEndpoints`: every running `OpenRequestServer` lists itself in `<system>/agenstorm/endpoints/` (one file per project folder, `port<TAB>token<TAB>folder`, the folder `rwx------`, each file `rw-------` and written whole) and unlists itself when it stops; a previous run's files are cleared on this run's first write. Both shims now go through `send`/`try`: the endpoint from their environment first, then the listed ones — the project whose folder holds `$PWD` first — passing a request on only when an endpoint cannot be reached (curl 7/28) or does not know the token (403); a 409, a 503 or a dropped edit still goes to the real `open` / the fallback editor, never to another window. The list sits beside the shims (`${0%/*}/../endpoints`), so no variable is needed. 6 new tests (3 run the real edit shim against local servers: a gone endpoint hands over to the listed one, this folder's project first, a declined edit stays put); the shims' contract assertions updated; `check` green (1008) |

#### Phase U3 — Background terminals: kept on close and quit, seen, and back on reopen (decision 91)

A session running something whose only tabs belong to a project that closes, or to an IDE that quits, can be kept as a **background terminal**: `destroy-unattached` switched off for that one session and the session tagged `@agenstorm_background`. Nothing else ever outlives its tabs.

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| U3.1 | On project open (setting on), a tab for each background terminal of its project, which then stops being one (`destroy-unattached` back on) | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Quit* row). `TmuxReattach` (a `postStartupActivity`): with background terminals kept from this project, it makes the Terminal tool window's content — which is what sets the platform's restore of the last run's tabs off (`TerminalToolWindowFactory.createToolWindowContent`, async) — waits 2 s, and opens an attach tab (named as the kept tab was, `@agenstorm_name`, else what runs) for each one no tab shows or asks for by then, showing the tool window without taking focus. Matched on the **project**, not the repository as first written: two worktrees of one repository reopening after a quit each get their own back, the rest stay listed (U3.3). `TmuxTabsListener` now (1) settles any tab's background session once its client is there — `settled` also unsets `@agenstorm_background` — whoever opened the tab (the platform's restore, U3.1, U3.3's *Open*), and (2) closes a second tab of the window asking for a session another tab already shows, for a restore slower than the wait. `TmuxTabs` holds the tab helpers the hand-off and the reattach share; `TmuxSessions` reads `@agenstorm_name`. 4 tests (2 plan, 1 real-tmux: kept → attached → settled → ends with its tab, 1 parser); verifier Compatible, zero internal (deprecated 4/12, experimental 176); `check` green (1014). **Guardrail run fix** (`a3a539c`): restored tabs start only once shown, so a reopened project's restored tab for its background terminal stayed unstarted and the list kept counting it — the reattach now selects the first kept session's tab, a restored one included, and shows the tool window; the list leaves out sessions a tab of an open window asks for |
| U3.2 | Keep or stop. **Project close:** U1.6's listener shows the platform's dialog for each tmux tab running something whose tabs are all in that project; *Disconnect* keeps it as a background terminal, *Terminate* ends it. **Quit:** one prompt of our own through `ApplicationListener.canExitApplication()` (public) — "2 terminals still running (claude in `app`, npm run dev in `fix-login`): *Keep Running* / *Stop All* / *Cancel*" — after which the per-tab listeners stay silent. A worktree switch asks nothing: U2.2 has already attached its running sessions in the target | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Quit* and *Project close* rows). `TmuxCloseGuard` (app service). **Project close** — `TmuxProjectCloseHandler` on `com.intellij.projectCloseHandler`: the sessions at stake (`TmuxBackgroundPlan.atStake`: running, no client beyond this window's tabs, still tagged with this project — a switch has retagged what it handed on) get the platform's dialog with *Disconnect* (`TmuxCloseDialog.ask`, `canKeep`). **Quit** — `canExitApplication` (an `ApplicationListener` added with `addApplicationListener(l, disposable)`, installed by `TmuxReattach` as projects open) asks once for every window: *Keep Running* / *Stop All* (*Stop* for one) / *Cancel*, labelled `claude in fix-login`, under the same remembered *Don't ask again* (`GeneralSettings.processCloseConfirmation`). Both only *record* the answer; it is acted on in `ProjectCloseListener.projectClosing` — after every veto, tabs still attached — for the sessions that window's tabs show: keep = `destroy-unattached off` + `@agenstorm_background 1` + `@agenstorm_name` (the tab's name, for U3.1), stop = `kill-session`. An answer counts for a minute, as the platform's own check does. The verifier caught `AppLifecycleListener.appStarted` and `beforeAppWillBeClosed` as `@Internal` (method-level), so the quit's answer is applied window by window at `projectClosing` instead of once before the windows close. **Agenstorm's own closes ask nothing and keep** — `core/QuietClose` wraps the worktree switch's, offloading's (Epic P) and a removal's `closeAndDispose`: since U2.3 a tmux tab is never busy, so an idle project with an agent in a tmux tab was offloadable and its agent ended with it; now it is kept as a background terminal and returns as a tab when the project loads again (U3.1). Also: ✕ → *Terminate* kills the session outright (a background terminal not settled yet has `destroy-unattached` off), and listing a project's tabs — the close guard, and `TerminalCommandGuard` on every offload sweep — no longer makes the Terminal tool window's content when it has none (`getContentManagerIfCreated`), which set off the platform's tab restore, shells and all, in projects nobody had opened a terminal in. 9 tests (4 plan, 2 decision, 1 quit buttons, 2 real-tmux: kept under its tab's name then stopped; exact targets); verifier Compatible, zero internal (deprecated 4/12, experimental 183); `check` green (1022) |
| U3.3 | *N background terminals*, shown only when there are any, in two places: a button in the Terminal tool window's title bar (next to the maximize toggle) and a status bar widget. Both open one list — what runs, the worktree, since when — with *Open* (a tab in this window when the repository matches, else opens its project) and *Stop* per row, *Stop All* at the bottom | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Project close* and *Quit* rows). `BackgroundTerminals` (app service): the sessions tagged background with no client (`TmuxBackgroundPlan.listed`, oldest first), read every 10 s while the setting is on and at once after a keep, a stop, a settle or an unattached hand-off; a change bumps `ActivityTracker` for the button and updates each window's widget (`StatusBarWidgetsManager.updateWidget` when the count crosses zero). `BackgroundTerminalsAction` (`Agenstorm.BackgroundTerminals`, after the maximize toggle in `TerminalToolwindowActionGroup`, text shown in the toolbar) and `BackgroundTerminalsWidgetFactory` (text widget) open `BackgroundTerminalsPopup`: a row per terminal — `✳ Claude Code — fix-login · since Today 13:05` — with *Open* and *Stop*, then *Stop All*. *Open* (`TmuxBackgroundPlan.openIn`): this window when the session is of its repository, or its folder is gone — the session is then re-tagged to this project (`adopt`) and gets a tab, or the window's tab already asking for it is selected; otherwise its own project's window, focused if open, else opened in a new frame, where U3.1 gives it its tab. A hand-off session no tab attached to is now kept with its tab's name too. 2 tests (listing, *Open*'s rule); verifier Compatible, zero internal, deprecated 4/14 (+2 on 263: `OpenProjectTask.build()`, as in `ProjectLoader`), experimental 185; `check` green (1024) |
| U3.4 | Cleanup: a background terminal whose program has ended (the pane is back at its shell) is killed when next seen; at IDE start, every session on the socket that is not a background terminal is a leftover (a crash) and is killed | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail's *Cleanup* row). Every `BackgroundTerminals` read stops the background terminals whose panes are back at their shells (`TmuxBackgroundPlan.ended`) before listing; the run's first read also stops `leftovers` — not background, no client, made before this JVM started (`RuntimeMXBean.startTime`) — so this run's own sessions, mid hand-off or not, and another running IDE's attached ones on the same socket are never touched. Nothing busy is ever ended on a timer. 2 tests; `check` green (1026) |

### Epic V — Soft wrap at the right margin  ·  after Epic U

**Goal:** in Markdown and text files, soft wrap stops at the right margin — the vertical line of the recommended line length — or at the window's edge when the window is narrower, never past the line. Today soft wrap follows the window width only, so on a wide window with a small font a paragraph runs well past the line.

**Success metrics:** in a soft-wrapped Markdown file with the right margin at 120, no visual line is wider than 120 columns however wide the window; narrower than that, lines wrap at the window's edge as today; zooming the font, changing the margin in Code Style and resizing the window re-wrap without reopening the file; nothing changes in files outside the IDE's soft-wrap list, in diff views, consoles or the terminal; zero internal API, `verifyPlugin` Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263.

Roman, 2026-10-01: "currently when soft-wrap is turned on, it wraps at window width; if window width is big enough and font is small enough, the text goes beyond vertical line of recommended line length. What I want is to wrap at this vertical line or less, but not more." VS Code calls it `editor.wordWrap: "bounded"`. Asked in the brainstorm the same day he chose: **Markdown and text only**, recognised through the IDE's own *Soft-wrap these files* list, and approved the design below (decision 95).

Platform facts (checked 2026-10-01 in PS-262.10968.76 and PS-263.5701.46):

- **The platform does this already — in Distraction-Free mode only.** `SoftWrapApplianceManager$DefaultVisibleAreaWidthProvider.getVisibleAreaWidth()` returns the visible width minus insets and, when `editor.isInDistractionFreeMode()`, `min(width, settings.getRightMargin(project) × EditorUtil.getPlainSpaceWidth(editor))` — exactly the rule wanted.
- **The width is replaceable per editor:** `SoftWrapApplianceManager.setWidthProvider(VisibleAreaWidthProvider)` and `getWidthProvider()`, reached through `SoftWrapModelImpl.getApplianceManager()` (the editor's `softWrapModel` cast). None of the three carries `@ApiStatus.Internal` in 262 or 263 (`SoftWrapModelImpl` is `@NonExtendable` — we only call it; its `setSoftWrapPainter` *is* internal and is not needed). 263's `ExperimentalSoftWrapModelImpl` goes through the same appliance manager (its `SoftWrappingEnabledRecalculationManager`), as does `LegacySoftWrapModelImpl`. They are `impl` classes: not internal, but not API either — §2 lists them, §5 carries the drift risk.
- **Public helpers:** `EditorSettingsExternalizable.getSoftWrapFileMasks()` (by default `*.md; *.txt; *.rst; *.adoc`), `EditorUtil.getPlainSpaceWidth(editor)`, `EditorEx.reinitSettings()`, `Editor.getEditorKind()`, the `editorFactoryListener` EP (Epic F uses it).
- **Not known yet — the V0 spike's question:** what recalculates the wraps once the provider is swapped (`reinitSettings`, `recalculateAll`, or a visible-area change), and whether a font zoom, a right-margin change in Code Style, a resize and the soft-wrap toggle each re-ask the provider.

Package `softwrap/`, registered in `plugin.xml` (no optional dependency: Markdown-the-plugin is not needed, only the file list).

#### Phase V0 — Spike: does a swapped width provider wrap at the margin?

Throwaway code in the robot IDE; each answer recorded here.

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| V0.1 | A provider capping the platform's own at `rightMargin × plainSpaceWidth`, set on a Markdown editor's appliance manager; which call makes the wraps follow (`EditorEx.reinitSettings`, `recalculateAll`, a resize) | ✅ | 2026-10-01, robot IDE (PS-263), no plugin code: the provider was a `java.lang.reflect.Proxy` set from the robot's JS on `demo-wt/other/long.md` (eight 760-character paragraphs), the widest visual line measured in columns. **The problem reproduces:** margin 120, window 1600 px, tool windows hidden — lines wrapped at 178 columns at font 13, at 288 at font 9. **The provider works; no call is needed — or allowed.** The appliance manager caches the width (`myAvailableWidth`) and re-reads the provider only in `updateAvailableArea()`, which it runs on every visible-area event (its own `VisibleAreaListener`: a scroll, a resize) and which `EditorImpl` runs when the component first shows and on a font change; `updateAvailableArea`, `recalculateAll`, `recalculate` and `reset` are **`@Internal`** (`setWidthProvider`, which calls `reset()` itself, and `getWidthProvider` are not). So swapping the provider on an open editor changes nothing until the next such event (`reinitSettings` and `recalculateAll` re-wrap with the cached width), while a provider set in `EditorFactoryListener.editorCreated` is read at the first layout: a freshly opened `long.txt` (`MAIN_EDITOR`) wrapped at 118 columns from the start |
| V0.2 | What re-asks the provider afterwards: font zoom (Ctrl+wheel and *Reset font size*), the right margin changed in Code Style (Markdown and *Other file types*), a window resize, soft wrap toggled off and on, a split of the same file, Distraction-Free mode | ✅ | Same run, the cap in place. **Followed by themselves:** font size 9 → 12 → 9 (119, 118, 119 columns — the margin in pixels moves with the space width); the window 1600 → 1500 → 700 → 1600 px (119, 119, then 108 at the edge of a window narrower than the margin, 119); soft wrap off and on again (119). **Silent:** the right margin set to 80 in Code Style (`CodeStyleSettingsManager.notifyCodeStyleSettingsChanged()`): the editor and the provider both read 80 at once, the wraps stayed at 120 — until a scroll of 1 px and back (public `ScrollingModel.scrollVertically`), after which 79, and 119 again at 120; and putting the original provider back (the off switch): 119 until the same nudge, then 288. The nudge needs a document taller than the window; one that fits keeps the old width until its next resize, zoom or reopen. A split opens a new editor (the creation path); Distraction-Free mode was not run — the original provider, which the cap wraps, applies the margin there itself. Log: no SEVERE |
| V0.3 | Go / no-go and the recalculation recipe recorded; decision row if it changes the plan | ✅ | **Go.** The recipe: set the provider in `editorCreated` (nothing to call); for an editor already open — the feature switched on or off, a margin changed in Code Style — swap or keep the provider, then nudge the scroll by 1 px and back. Never `reinitSettings`/`recalculateAll` (useless) nor `updateAvailableArea` (internal). No plan change beyond V1.2/V1.3's wording, so no decision row. Throwaway: the driver `u0/v0.py` in the session scratchpad, nothing in the repository |

**Exit guardrails — Phase V0**

| Guardrail | Criteria (pass/fail) | Status | Actual outcome |
|-----------|----------------------|--------|----------------|
| Go / no-go | V0.1 wraps at the margin on a wide window and at the edge on a narrow one; V0.2 names a public trigger for each change or a listener that can call one | ✅ | Go (2026-10-01): at the margin on a wide window (119 of 120 columns), at the edge on a narrow one (108); zoom and resize follow by themselves; a margin change and the off switch follow a 1-px scroll nudge (public), which a listener can make — a document shorter than the window waits for its next resize or zoom |

#### Phase V1 — Wrap at the margin

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| V1.1 | `softwrap/MarginWrap`, pure: `width(visible, rightMarginColumns, spaceWidth)` = `min(visible, columns × spaceWidth)` when the margin is positive, else `visible`; `matches(masks, fileName)` over the IDE's `;`-separated soft-wrap masks (`*`, `?`, case as the platform matches). Tests | ✅ | 2026-10-01. The platform's own mask matching is not reachable through public API (only the settings panel reads `getSoftWrapFileMasks()` directly), so `matches` is a glob of its own: `;` or `,` separated, whole name, ignoring case as file-type extensions do. 2 tests |
| V1.2 | `MarginWidthProvider(editor, original)`: the original's width capped by V1.1, the margin read on every call (so it follows the language's code style); `MarginWrapService` (app service) + an `EditorFactoryListener`: a `MAIN_EDITOR` of a file the soft-wrap list matches gets the provider in `editorCreated` (read at the first layout, V0.1), the original kept in the editor's user data; switching on or off swaps the provider of every open matching editor and nudges its scroll by 1 px and back (V0.2). Diff views, consoles, the terminal and files outside the list are never touched; a failed cast or call logs once and leaves the editor as it was | ✅ | 2026-10-01 (built; the feel is the guardrail run). `MarginWidthProvider` implements the appliance manager's `VisibleAreaWidthProvider` around the editor's own; `MarginWrapService` (app service): `attach` from `MarginWrapEditorListener` (`editorFactoryListener`, `plugin.xml`) for a `MAIN_EDITOR` whose file the soft-wrap list matches; `applyToOpenEditors` on `AgenstormSettingsListener` swaps every open editor's provider either way and nudges its scroll; the cap holds the original, so no user data is needed; a `LinkageError` from the `impl` classes logs once and leaves the editor alone. The test fixture's own editor is `UNTYPED`, so the tests make `MAIN_EDITOR`, `DIFF` and `CONSOLE` editors with `EditorFactory.createEditor` — which also runs the registered listener. 5 tests; verifier Compatible, zero internal (deprecated 4/14, experimental 183 — the soft-wrap classes add nothing to either list)  |
| V1.3 | Re-wrap on a right-margin change: a Code Style settings listener nudges every capped editor's scroll (V0.2); zoom, resize and the soft-wrap toggle need nothing | ✅ | 2026-10-01. `MarginWrapCodeStyleListener` (`CodeStyleSettingsListener`, a project listener in `plugin.xml`): on a Code Style change, the project's capped editors are nudged, under `ModalityState.any()` so they re-wrap while the settings dialog is still open |
| V1.4 | Setting `softWrapAtRightMargin` (on by default) — *Soft-wrap Markdown and text files at the right margin, never past it* — in its own *Soft wraps* group, the comment naming the IDE's *Soft-wrap these files* list; applies to open editors at once | ✅ | 2026-10-01. A *Soft wraps* group before *Status bar*: *Soft-wrap Markdown and text files at the right margin, never past it*, its comment naming Code Style's *Hard wrap at* and the *Soft-wrap these files* list; applying fires `AgenstormSettingsListener`, which re-wraps the open editors; 1 test (on by default); `check` green (1045) |
| V1.5 | README + CHANGELOG; §2 row; verifier (zero internal) | ✅ | 2026-10-01. README: a sentence in the plugin description's Markdown paragraph, a row in the features table, a *Soft wrap at the right margin* section (the rule, the files, what stays alone, the short-file caveat); CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` *Added*; §2's internal-API row has carried the soft-wrap provider since the epic was spec'd; verifier Compatible, zero internal |

**Exit guardrails — Phase V1**

|-----------|----------------------|--------|----------------|
| Bounded | A Markdown file of long paragraphs, soft wrap on, right margin 120: on a wide window with a small font no line passes the margin's vertical line; on a window narrower than the margin lines wrap at the edge as before | ✅ | 2026-10-01, robot IDE (PS-263), one launch with the plugin itself. `demo-wt/other/long.md` opened fresh in a 1600-px window, tool windows hidden: the widest visual line was 118 columns at font 13 and 119 at font 9 (margin 120) — where the platform alone gave 178 and 288 in V0; a 700-px window wrapped at its edge (108 columns), 1600 px again at 119 |
| Follows | Font zoom in and out, the margin changed to 80 in Code Style, the window resized: the wraps follow each time without reopening the file | ✅ | Same run, the window 500 px high so the file scrolls: the Markdown margin set to 80 in Code Style → 79 columns, back to 120 → 119 (the Code Style listener's nudge); font 12 → 118, 9 → 119; the resizes above |

### Epic W — Markdown contents in the corner  ·  after 1.11.0

**Goal:** a Markdown editor shows the file's headings in its top-right corner — a contents card that stays put while the text scrolls, highlights the section on screen and jumps to a heading on a click — and folds itself into a small ☰ pill when there is no room for it beside the text.

**Success metrics:** in a Markdown file with two or more headings the card is in the corner at any scroll position; a click lands on the heading and Back returns; the highlighted entry follows scrolling; a heading typed in shows up within half a second; on a window too narrow for the card beside the right margin it is a pill, and widening the window brings the card back without reopening the file; nothing appears in PHP or other editors, in diffs, or in the preview pane; zero internal API, `verifyPlugin` Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263.

Roman, 2026-10-07: "Let's add contents widget in markdown files on the right, that is always sticky on the top". Asked in the brainstorm the same day he chose: the card when it fits beside the text and a pill otherwise (over always-a-card and always-a-pill), H1–H3 by default with a depth setting (over every level and H2–H3 only), the editor's floating-toolbar slot as the host (over a layered-pane child of our own and an anchored popup), and on by default — decisions 96 and 97.

Platform facts (checked 2026-10-07 in PS-262.8665.265 and PS-263.5701.46 by decompiling the Gradle cache's copies):

- **The editor already has a top-right slot that does not scroll.** `EditorImpl`'s layered pane (`PanelWithFloatingToolbar`, private) holds an `EditorFloatingToolbar` at layer 300 — above sticky lines (200) and the vertical scrollbar (250) — and its `doLayout` puts it at `width − preferredWidth − scrollbarWidth − 20, 20`, outside the scroll pane, so it stays where it is while the text moves. The slot itself is a `FlowLayout(RIGHT, 20, 20)`, whose gaps add 20 px more on each side: a toolbar inside it sits 40 px plus the scrollbar from the right edge and 40 px from the top (found by the Epic W review; W1.3's rule counts it). It is made once per editor on first show, for editors that are not one-line, not a diff and have a file (`mayShowToolbar`).
- **It is filled from a public extension point.** `com.intellij.editorFloatingToolbarProvider` (dynamic) with `FloatingToolbarProvider` (`@OverrideOnly`, not internal), the same member list in 262 and 263: `getActionGroup`, `isApplicableAsync(DataContext)`, `register(DataContext, FloatingToolbarComponent, Disposable)`, `onHiddenByEsc(DataContext)` (`@Experimental`), `autoHideable`, `backgroundAlpha` and the three timings. JetBrains' own Gradle-reload and HotSwap buttons use it.
- **`isApplicableAsync` is asked once, when the editor's toolbar is made.** So the provider answers yes for every Markdown file editor, and the on/off setting and the heading count are the action's business: after every action update the toolbar runs `setVisible(isVisible && hasVisibleActions())`, which is what lets the settings page switch it in open editors.
- **Each provider becomes an `ActionToolbarImpl`** (`AbstractFloatingToolbarComponent`, place `ContextToolbar`, `NOWRAP_STRATEGY`), so a `CustomComponentAction` can put any Swing component in it. The toolbar fills a rounded rectangle at `backgroundAlpha × opacity` — alpha 0 paints nothing, leaving the card's look to us — and paints its children at the animator's opacity, 1 once shown.
- **Esc hides it.** A key listener on the editor calls `hideImmediately()` on every floating toolbar on Esc and then `onHiddenByEsc`; with `autoHideable = false` only `scheduleShow()` brings it back, so the provider calls it from there.
- **A column of its own is closed.** `TextEditorComponent` (itself `@Internal`) throws from both `add(…)` overloads and its `__add` is `@Internal` — the minimap's old way in is gone; `EditorImpl.addLayer`, the editor's own custom-layer hook, is `@Internal`; a plain child of the editor's layered pane is laid out as the inspection widget — pinned to the edge, the scrollbar pushed below it.
- **Headings come from the Markdown PSI.** `MarkdownHeader.getLevel()` (public) exists in both builds (`buildVisibleText` was checked and is not used: it keeps emphasis markers and backticks); a `#` line inside fenced code or front matter never parses into a `MarkdownHeader`. `IdeDocumentHistory.includeCurrentCommandAsNavigation()` (public) is what makes a jump a step Back can undo.

#### Phase W1 — The outline

| Step | Description | Status | Notes |
|------|-------------|--------|-------|
| W1.1 | `markdown/toc/TocEntry` + `TocCollector`: the file's `MarkdownHeader`s → `TocEntry(level, title, line, offset)` in a read action, the title walked from the header's content node with whitespace collapsed, empty headings skipped | ✅ | 2026-10-07, 4 tests (fixture `toc.md`: ATX 1–6, setext 1–2, `#` lines in a fence, indented code and front matter ignored, `**bold**`, `` `code` ``, `~~strike~~` and `[text](url)` as plain text, empty and image-only headings skipped; after the Epic W review also inline HTML such as `<img …>` left out and `<https://…>` autolinks without their brackets). The text is walked from the content node because `buildVisibleText` keeps `**` and backticks; only delimiters inside an emphasis, strike, code or link element are dropped, so an unmatched `*` stays. Front matter is skipped by offset (`TocCollector.frontMatterEnd`) whatever the Markdown plugin's setting |
| W1.2 | `TocOutline`, pure: `visible(entries, depth)`, `shows(visible)` (two or more), `current(visible, topLine)` — the last entry at or above the viewport's top line, none above the first | ✅ | 2026-10-07, 4 unit tests, including a viewport between two headings, one before the first, and a file whose headings are all deeper than the depth (nothing shows) |
| W1.3 | `TocFit`, pure: `mode(viewportWidth, marginColumns, spaceWidth, cardWidth, rightInset)` → `CARD` when `viewportWidth − marginColumns × spaceWidth ≥ cardWidth + rightInset + 20 px`, where `rightInset` is the slot's distance from the edge — the scrollbar's width, the editor's 20 px and the slot's own `FlowLayout` gap, read from the layout — else `PILL`; a margin of 0 or less means `PILL` | ✅ | 2026-10-07, 4 unit tests: wide, narrow, the exact threshold, no margin. The margin is what Epic V's soft wrap keeps clear; with soft wrap off a long line can still run under the card (risk register) |
