## 7. Open questions

- [x] ~~**2026.3 EAP 263.6259 does not resolve the VCS classes (found 2026-10-07 by W2.6's verifier run):**~~ **Resolved 2026-10-07 — a verifier false positive, ignored until a verifier release catches up** (details below). PS-263.6259.29 and IU-263.6259.32 report 14 compatibility problems — `VcsRepositoryManager`, `VcsDataKeys`, `CommitWorkflowUi`, `CurrentContentRevision`, `IdeaTextPatchBuilder`, `UnifiedDiffWriter`, `VcsDirtyScopeManager`, `ProjectLevelVcsManager`, `CommitMessage`, `DvcsStatusWidget` — referenced from `commit/`, `worktrees/` and `tabs/git/`. 263.5701 (1.11.0's) was Compatible. Likely the VCS split CLAUDE.md describes going further (the classes now living in a module the plugin does not declare); needs a look at where they moved and whether `plugin.xml` needs a module dependency before the next release. Not Epic W's: none of its files are involved. **Checked 2026-10-07:** the classes all still exist in 263.6259 — what moved is `intellij.platform.vcs` (`VcsDataKeys`, `CommitWorkflowUi`), from the core `lib/` into the bundled *Version Control* plugin, whose id also changed (`intellij.vcs.plugin` → `com.intellij.platform.vcs`); `vcs.impl` and `dvcs.impl` were already in that plugin. The `com.intellij.modules.vcs` alias our `plugin.xml` depends on is still declared, by that plugin's `intellij.platform.vcs.impl` content module, in both builds. Plugin Verifier 1.410 — the newest release, 2026-08-26, older than this EAP — resolves the alias as a core *product module* and finds no classes behind it, so every VCS class reads as missing: a verifier limitation, not a missing dependency. Evidence: with `platformVersion` switched to 263.6259.29 the plugin compiles and all 1086 tests pass (switched back afterwards). Not yet checked: a runtime smoke test in a 263.6259 sandbox (project open with Git, the commit toolbar, the branch widget, the worktree strip). Until a verifier release understands the move, `build.yml`'s `verifyPlugin` fails on any push, since `recommended()` now picks 263.6259; `pluginVerification { ignoredProblemsFile }` can list these 14 problems with a pointer here. **Runtime smoke test, same day, on Roman's "do both":** the robot IDE on PS-263.6259.29 with the Git demo project — every one of the twelve flagged classes loads through Agenstorm's own classloader (served by the Version Control plugin's content modules `intellij.platform.vcs`, `.vcs.impl`, `.vcs.dvcs`, `.vcs.dvcs.impl`), and so do Agenstorm's classes that use them (`GenerateCommitMessageAction`, `DiffCollector`, `BranchStartupActivity`, `WorktreeRegistry`, `MergeBackFlow`, `BranchStatusBarWidget`); the repository is found, the Commit tool window opens, the branch widget is in the status bar as the `GitBranchWidget` subclass; the log has no error, nothing blamed on Agenstorm and no missing class (the robot server's own failed shutdown aside). **Done:** `verifier-ignored-problems.txt` at the root, wired through `pluginVerification { ignoredProblemsFile }`, holds two patterns — *package … is not found* and *access to unresolved class …* for `com.intellij.dvcs`, `.vcs`, `.openapi.vcs` and `.openapi.diff.impl.patch` — and `verifyPlugin` is Compatible on PS/IU 262 and 263.6259 again, zero internal. Remove the file once a verifier release follows the alias into the plugin.
- [x] ~~**Epic P, `TerminalCommandGuard` on the classic engine (seen 2026-09-22 in the I1.3 sandbox):**~~ **Fixed the same day** (`0f5f791`, `c802235`), forced by Epic I's *Classic engine* guardrail: the classic widget is asked nothing on the EDT — its `isCommandRunning()` is `hasRunningCommands()` too — the last background answer serves, and *busy* until there is one. with a classic JediTerm tab open, the offload sweep's `isRunning` trips two `ThreadingAssertions` SEVEREs blamed on Agenstorm — `TerminalWidget.isCommandRunning()` asserts a background thread and `ShellTerminalWidget.hasRunningCommands()` asserts no read access — because `Offloader.sweep` runs on the EDT under the write-intent lock. 24 in one session, none ever in Roman's daily-driver log (reworked engine only). Fix: ask the guards off the EDT ahead of the sweep, or drop the classic branch. Not urgent; a classic-engine user would see it.
- [x] ~~**Epic O, Phase O2 (duplicate / paste-a-copy):** through the `com.intellij.refactoring.copyHandler` EP or through an `ActionSlot` on `CopyElement`?~~ **Resolved 2026-09-14 — neither** (decision 57). O2 adds its own `Agenstorm.DuplicateInline` action, so `CopyElement` keeps its dialog and its target-directory browser. The EP was checked and would have been safe (`CopyHandler.doCopy` is first-wins, no chooser, unlike `RenameHandlerRegistry`), but an action that takes nothing over does not need it. Paste-into-the-same-folder is out: `$Paste` has no seam that reaches only the tree.
- [ ] Default models per backend at implementation time (Anthropic and OpenAI model ids change; pick the current mid-tier default and keep it a free-text setting).
- [ ] Should `DiffCollector` honour a project-level ignore file (e.g. `.aiignore` / `.agenstormignore`) for files that must never leave the machine? Default: no, keep v1 simple; revisit after daily use.
- [ ] Does `claude -p` on the author's machine accept `--max-turns 1` / `--tools ""` style flags to guarantee a tool-free single response? Verify during D2.3; otherwise rely on the prompt.
- [x] **Epic D, clearing the message box after a commit — settled 2026-09-10, no plugin code.** Roman: "after commit has been committed, cleanup commit message text box". The platform already does it. `AbstractCommitMessagePolicy.onAfterCommit()` sets the field to `CommitMessage.EMPTY` and calls `cleanupStoredMessage()` when `VcsConfiguration.CLEAR_INITIAL_COMMIT_MESSAGE` is on; otherwise it refills the field from the changelist description, which `ChangeListCommitMessagePolicy` has been writing the typed message into all along — that is why the just-committed message reappears. The flag is Settings | Version Control | Commit → "Clear initial commit message", off by default, and in build 262 `AbstractCommitMessagePolicy` is its only reader with behaviour attached (the other three hits are `CommitDialogConfigurable`, `VcsOptionsTopHitProvider` and `VcsOptionsUsagesCollector`), so turning it on does this and nothing else. Roman ticks the checkbox; Agenstorm neither surfaces nor forces the flag.
- [x] ~~**Live markup, reveal granularity:** the caret policy reveals every hidden marker on the caret line; Obsidian and the other hybrid editors reveal inline elements one at a time.~~ Resolved 2026-09-06: Phase F3, decision 24.
- [ ] Should live markup also render images (`![alt](src)`) — as `🖼 alt` placeholder or as a block inlay? Deferred; not in 1.0. Epic H deliberately left this out when it took on fences, quotes and rules.
- [ ] Should Epic H also give indented code blocks (`MarkdownElementTypes.CODE_BLOCK`) the card treatment? They need no markers hidden, so it is only the background — cheap once H1 exists. Left out of 1.0 on purpose; revisit after use.
- [ ] **Epic I, external-command rules:** should a rule be allowed to name a command that the matched block is piped through, its output replacing the block? Rejected for 1.0 by decision 28 (arbitrary execution over terminal output). If it comes back, it needs an explicit opt-in toggle that is off by default, a per-rule confirmation on first use, and a note in the Marketplace description.
- [x] **Epic G, `ProjectUtil` API status — settled 2026-09-06, not internal.** The class-level `RuntimeVisibleAnnotations` of `com.intellij.ide.impl.ProjectUtil` is `kotlin.Metadata` alone; the `@ApiStatus.Internal` in the class file attaches to exactly two members, `openExistingDir(Path, FolderOpeningMode, Project, …)` and `isValidProjectPathAsync(Path, …)`. `focusProjectWindow`, `findAndFocusExistingProjectForPath`, `openOrImportAsync` and `isSameProject` carry no ApiStatus at all, so G2.1 and G2.2 use them as public API.
- [ ] **Epic G on Windows:** the shim is POSIX `sh`, so `TerminalOpenExecOptionsCustomizer` no-ops on Windows. Worth an `open.cmd` / PowerShell function later? Default: no, macOS-first (same stance as Epic A's drive paths).
- [ ] **Epic G outside the IDE:** should the plugin offer to install the shim into the user's own shell profile, so `open path:line` works in iTerm too? It would need a stable port or a discovery file, and it takes the "only inside IDE terminals" safety argument away. Default: no.
- [ ] Epic E on Windows/Linux: the widget works, but is it wanted there (native tabs do not exist)? Default: available, off by default outside macOS.
- [ ] Should `Copy Location Link` also offer `path:line:col` relative to the *repository* root vs. content root when they differ (monorepos)? Default: content root; decide after use.
- [x] ~~**Epic B, dialects:** the scratch popup lists *languages*, but `scratchLanguageFilter` filters by *file type*; JavaScript dialects map to the JavaScript file type and stay whenever JavaScript is allowed.~~ Resolved 2026-09-05: accept and document (decision 14). **Fixed 2026-09-10** (decision 39): the popup is ours and filters languages, so ActionScript and ECMAScript 6 stay out unless named.
- [ ] **Auto-hiding notification balloons — feasibility settled 2026-09-10, nothing built.** Roman asked whether notification popups can be hidden automatically. The zero-code half: Settings | Appearance & Behavior | Notifications carries a global "Display balloon notifications" (`NotificationsConfigurationImpl.SHOW_BALLOONS`) and a per-group display type (`No popups` / `Balloon` / `Sticky balloon` / `Tool window`); `No popups` still logs the entry to the Notifications tool window, and `Balloon` fades by itself while `Sticky balloon` does not — which is the part that actually annoys. The plugin half is all public API: `NotificationsConfiguration.setDisplayType(groupId, NONE)` (the abstract class carries no ApiStatus at all, and `NotificationsConfigurationImpl` carries only `@State`, so neither is internal — same reasoning as the `ProjectUtil` row above), and for a timed auto-dismiss a `Notifications.TOPIC` subscriber calling `Notification.hideBalloon()` (popup gone, entry kept) or `expire()` (entry dropped) after a delay. Two traps to design around: `Notifications.TOPIC` is declared `BroadcastDirection.NONE` and `Notifications.Bus.doNotify` publishes project-scoped notifications on the project bus and the rest on the app bus, so a listener needs both an `applicationListeners` entry and a per-project subscription or it sees half of them; and at `notify()` time the balloon does not exist yet (`getBalloon()` is null), so the hide must be scheduled, never inline — "never show at all" belongs to the display type instead. The platform's own timer (`BalloonLayoutData.fadeoutTime` → `BalloonImpl.startSmartFadeoutTimer`) sits behind `NotificationsManagerImpl` and is not reachable, so a plugin schedules its own hide rather than retuning theirs. Roman chose the timed auto-dismiss the same day; it is **Epic L**, and the platform facts moved there.
- [x] **The right tool window bar — settled 2026-09-13 as a trade, not a win.** Hiding the stripe icons in place is internal-API-only in build 262 (decision 48); Epic N ships the re-anchoring version instead (decision 49), which empties the bar but moves those windows to the left. **Still worth asking JetBrains** for a public per-side lever — a counterpart to `UISettings.hideToolStripes`, or a non-internal "remove from sidebar" — which would let the windows stay on the right with no icons; the same route produced IJPL-221866 for Epic K. Also worth a re-check on 263: the New UI's stripe code is still moving (`setShowStripeButton` already a no-op, `setVisibleOnLargeStripe` dead with zero callers).
- [ ] **`Markdown.Toolbar.Right` looks dead in build 262 — found while surveying Epic M, 2026-09-12.** The Markdown plugin declares the group in its `plugin.xml` (holding `AutoScrollAction`), and `Agenstorm.ToggleLiveMarkup` is added to it, but the id is referenced by **no class in the whole distribution**: every jar under `lib/`, `plugins/*/lib/` and `plugins/*/lib/modules/` was scanned for the string and the only hits are that `plugin.xml` and the searchable-options index. `MarkdownEditorWithPreview` overrides neither `createLeftToolbarActionGroup` nor `createRightToolbarActionGroup`, which are what `TextEditorWithPreview` would consult, and its only overrides are `onLayoutChange`, `requestFocusForPreview` and the auto-scroll pair. If that is right, the Live Markup button never renders in the editor toolbar and only the context-menu entry works — which would also make `settings.markdown.bullets.comment` ("The Live Markup button in a Markdown editor's toolbar…") untrue. **Needs one look in the sandbox before anything is changed**; the fix, if confirmed, is to move the action to a group that is alive.
- [ ] **Epic P on Windows/Linux:** nothing in offloading is macOS-specific — a project window is closed and reopened the same way, it just does not join a tab group. Default: available wherever the tabs are (the same stance as the Epic E row above).
- [ ] **Epic P, what counts as active:** P2.2 records frame activation only, so a project you type in for two hours without ever leaving it is "active" by the last activation, which is fine, and a project you *look at* through a second monitor without clicking it is not. Revisit after a week of use; the `IdeEventQueue` idle listener is the public lever if typing needs to count.
- [ ] **Epic Q, copy:** should the rendered table offer "Copy as Markdown" / "Copy as TSV" on its context menu? A selection across it already copies raw Markdown. Default: no; revisit after use.
- [ ] **Epic Q, width:** natural width capped at the viewport (GitHub's rendering), or stretched to the full editor width like the target screenshot? Q1.2 lays out natural; decide at the Q2 *Looks like the target* guardrail.
- [x] ~~**Epic T, strip placement:** a row above the Project tree (Roman's pick; needs the content wrapped) or the header row through `ProjectViewTabToolbar`?~~ **Resolved 2026-09-30 — above the tree, decision 82**: the Project view panel's own unused toolbar slot, no wrapping; the header row renders nothing until hovered.
- [ ] **Epic T, `.idea` seeding:** besides `ProjectId`, which `workspace.xml` components are per checkout and must not be copied (`ChangeListManager`, `TaskManager`, `RunManager`'s temporary configurations, shelf state)? Default: drop `ProjectId`, `ChangeListManager` and `TaskManager`; settle at the Phase T2 *Not a twin* guardrail.
- [ ] **Epic T, chunk freshness:** if T0 is a go, how far behind the worktree's commit can the main checkout's chunk be before a re-dump pays off? T0.3 measures one point (20 commits); pick N from it.
- [x] **Epic T, making an index chunk (T2.6 ⏸️) — decided 2026-09-30: route (c), left out for now (decision 85); (a) stays the way back in.** a third-party plugin may serve a chunk (`sharedIndexLocalFinder`, main module) but not make one in-process — the exporter lives in an `internal`-visibility content module and the project chunk in a private one. Routes: (a) the documented headless `dump-shared-index project` starter, run as a second IDE process with its own config and system dirs — public, but a whole IDE instance and a minute or two per `composer.lock`; (b) reflection through the `SharedIndexes.DumpSharedIndex` action's classloader, as T0.2 did — works, but reaches into internal modules, against §2; (c) leave index reuse out of Epic T and ship the rest. Recommendation: (c) now, (a) as its own step if the ~7 s per new worktree turns out to matter.
- [ ] **Epic T, the swap's blink:** the switch opens the target in a frame of its own and then closes the old one, so both show for a moment (Roman, T1 guardrail: acceptable for now). `OpenProjectTask.forceReuseFrame` with `projectToClose` would swap inside one frame, but it is only settable through the `OpenProjectTask { … }` builder, inline for JVM 25, and its getter is deprecated. Revisit once the plugin's JVM target moves, or find another public path.
- [x] ~~**Epic T, default:** is the feature on by default?~~ **Resolved 2026-09-30 — on (decision 83)**, Roman at the Phase T1 guardrail: "keep it on".
- [ ] **Epic T, switches nobody asked for:** in two robot sessions of the T3 run the strip switched on its own — app → tables-q → fix-login → tables-q at 17:54 (12 s after start), and app ↔ fix-login every ~1.5 s at 17:59, right after a second `runIdeForUiTests` handed the running instance an "open app" and rewrote the plugin jar under it. A clean session with a caller trace on every switch (`WorktreeSwitcher` at debug) had none, and an outside open of the main checkout while a worktree was showing just opened a second window. Unknown whether those were clicks; if it shows up again, the debug trace names the caller.
- [ ] **Epic T, edits in worktrees nobody opened:** their files are not in the VFS, so an edit there raises no event; the tab catches up on a git operation there, on the IDE coming to the front, or — for a live lock — on the 5–15 s poll. Loading each linked worktree's tracked folders into the VFS would give events for a price (and the platform applies outside changes only every 6–15 s anyway). Default: leave it; revisit if tabs feel stale in practice.
- [ ] **2026.3's Markdown plugin has its own live preview (found 2026-10-01 in a log check):** `intellij.markdown.frontend` ships `editor/livepreview/` — `MarkdownLivePreviewEditorListener` (an `editorFactoryListener` installed for every editor that `supportsMarkdown`), `MarkdownLivePreviewReconciler` (reveals markup at the caret), conceal, bullet, checkbox, horizontal-rule and image renderers, table-alignment inlays — which is much of Epics F, H and Q. Not checked: whether it is on by default and visible, whether it and live markup fight over the same ranges (both fold or conceal, both react to the caret), and whether Agenstorm should step aside where it runs. Its caret listener also throws, in Roman's `runIde` session of 2026-10-01 (moving the caret down in a Markdown file with a table): *Read access is allowed from inside read-action only* from `MarkdownLivePreviewReconciler.onCaretChanged` → `MarkdownTableAlignmentController.performRefresh` → `PsiElementBase.isValid`, blamed on Markdown 263.5701.46, over 50 times, no Agenstorm frame — the EAP's bug, but whether it also happens without Agenstorm loaded was not tried. Next step when someone picks it up: a 2026.3 run with Agenstorm disabled, then one with live markup off
- [ ] **Epic U, a program's title in a restored tab's name (seen in the U3 guardrail run):** the platform persists a tab's name as `userDefinedTitle ?: defaultTitle`, and `TmuxTitleMirror` (U1.7) puts the program's title in `defaultTitle` while it runs, so a tmux tab closed with Claude running comes back after a restart as a fresh shell named `✶ Claude Code` (one spinner frame), and a background terminal kept from such a restored, unstarted tab takes that name too. The persisting is sampled every 300 ms and gives no public hook before a close; ways out: restore the name when a restored tab starts (we know the mirror's frames), or keep `defaultTitle` alone and find another public lever for the title. Default: cosmetic, left for U4 or later
- [x] ~~**Epic U, tab names:** a moved tab is named after what runs in it, since a reworked tab's name cannot be read back through public API.~~ **Resolved 2026-10-01 — decision 92**: it can be read back (`TerminalView.getTitle().userDefinedTitle`, public), so a moved tab keeps a rename; and programs' own titles — Claude's animated one — show through tmux once the IDE is told something runs (U1.7, U2.2).
- [x] ~~**Epic U, scrollback:** tmux's own (copy mode) or kept in the IDE's buffer (`smcup@:rmcup@`)?~~ **Resolved 2026-10-01 by U0.5 — tmux's own**: `smcup@` loses lines (104 of 300 kept), the wheel scrolls tmux's history.
- [x] ~~**Epic U, what the busy guard sees:**~~ **Resolved 2026-10-01 by U2.3**: it saw no reworked tab, ever — `getTerminalWidgets()` lists only the old manager's — and now asks `TerminalToolWindowTabsManager` as well. in the U0 run `TerminalToolWindowManager.getTerminalWidgets()` listed no reworked tab — not the tab builder's, not one from the *New Tab* action invoked by the robot — so `TerminalCommandGuard` called a plain tab running `sleep` idle, while T1's *Busy* row passed in Roman's run. Settle which tabs it sees (and whether `TerminalToolWindowTabsManager.getTabs()` should be asked too) before U2.3 changes it.
- [ ] **Epic T on Windows/Linux:** the strip and swap are platform-neutral; the copy-on-write clone falls back to a plain copy where `cp -c` / `--reflink` is unavailable. Default: available everywhere, documented.

## How to Update This Document

This spec is the source of truth for the build. Keep it current as work happens:

- **Status markers.** Update a step's status in its tracker table as you go: 🔲 → 🔄 → ✅. Use ⏸️ for blocked (note why in Notes) and ❌ for cut (leave the row; the strikethrough of history is useful).
- **Current focus.** Keep the pointer at the top aimed at the next actionable 🔲 step. Update it the moment you finish a step or cross a phase boundary — a stale pointer is worse than none, since it sends the next reader to the wrong place.
- **Guardrails.** When you hit a phase boundary, fill the **Actual outcome** column with what really happened and set the guardrail status. Don't advance to the next phase until its entry guardrails pass — or log a decision explaining why you're proceeding anyway.
- **Decisions.** Any non-trivial choice made during the build gets a new row in the Decision Log (§6). It's append-only — reversals are new rows, not edits. If the choice changes the architecture, also update the Technical Decisions snapshot (§2).
- **Spec changes.** Structural changes (new epic, re-scoped phase) get a Changelog row at the top. Keep the executive summary honest if the project's shape shifts.
- **Open questions.** When one resolves, strike it from §7 and log the decision in §6.
